I just can't stand the idea of getting big.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is actually the shape and size that you see it as when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, when I look in the mirror... especially at my stomach and my waist...     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because the medical clinic for this logistics job I'm trying to get wouldn't clear me. They told me my weight was too low. \n\nI don't really agree with them, honestly. To me, I just look lean, you know? Like someone who's ready to work hard and stay quick on his feet. But they said I needed to come see a doctor about it if I want the job, so that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my sister and that clinic doctor keep saying I’m in some kind of danger, but I think they’re just making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. \n\nThey just don’t understand it. I'm just keeping myself light and sharp, you know? It keeps me ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word afraid, exactly, but... yeah, I definitely don't want to put on fat. \n\nLike, if I eat rice, or bread, or something greasy, I just... I can’t stop thinking about it. I feel like it’s going to just sit on me right away. It bothers me. So I’ll usually check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn’t let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it is. Normal for me, anyway. \n\nI don't really pay attention to those standard charts or whatever they use at the clinic. I've got a frame that's built for moving, not for carrying extra bulk around. So... yeah, for my height and the kind of work I do, I think it's perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I mean, I can't even remember the last time I sat down and ate a big meal, let alone a bunch of food all at once. I just stick to my routine. Small amounts, mostly just enough to keep me going. Eating a lot at once would just... it would feel heavy, you know? It would slow me down on the job. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time when you were alone?\n\nPatient: No, not even when I'm alone. I mean, my eating habits don't really change whether there's people around or not. I just don't have the desire to eat like that. It's just... small amounts, like I said. That's all I need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. Like I was saying, I don't eat heavy meals anyway, so there's really no reason I'd ever need to do something like that. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise a lot to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. My job is pretty physical as it is. I move freight and materials all day, so I'm constantly on my feet and lifting things. That's really all the workout I get. I don't go to a gym or run or anything on top of that just to burn calories. I'm usually pretty beat by the time I clock out anyway, so, no.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight interfere with your daily activities or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does get to me. I mean, if I eat something like rice, or bread, or anything greasy... I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just stays on my mind. And then the next morning, I always have to check the scale, or check how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of becoming overweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. I mean, like I was just saying... that's exactly why I get so worked up over eating bread or rice or anything greasy. The thought of it just sitting on my stomach and making me fat really scares me. That's why I have to do those checks with the scale and my belt every single morning. I just can't stand the idea of getting big.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is actually the shape and size that you see it as when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, when I look in the mirror... especially at my stomach and my waist... I don't know if I see what other people see. I just see that if I'm not smaller, if I don't look tight and lean, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. It's like my shape tells me if I'm keeping it together or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see your body as being the same shape and size as you see it when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, I don't go around asking the guys at the warehouse how they think I look. I just know what I see when I look at myself. So... yeah, I couldn't really tell you if they see the same thing or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what the scale says it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess so. A scale is just a scale, right? I don't really have any reason to think it's wrong or anything. It just gives me the number, and... well, I just go by that. So, yeah, I believe that's what I actually weigh.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I don't think I look underweight. My sister and my regular doctor, they keep telling me I'm in danger because of my size, but... I really think they're making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. They just don't understand it. I'm just keeping myself light, sharp, and ready for work. When you're moving freight all day at my age, you can't afford to be carrying extra bulk. So, no, I don't see myself as underweight, I just see a guy who's built for the job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

They told me my weight was too low. \n\nI don't really agree with them, honestly. To me, I just look lean, you know? Like someone who's ready to work hard and stay quick on his feet. But they said I needed to come see a doctor about it if I want the job, so that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my sister and that clinic doctor keep saying I’m in some kind of danger, but I think they’re just making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. \n\nThey just don’t understand it. I'm just keeping myself light and sharp, you know? It keeps me ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word afraid, exactly, but... yeah, I definitely don't want to put on fat. \n\nLike, if I eat rice, or bread, or something greasy, I just... I can’t stop thinking about it. I feel like it’s going to just sit on me right away. It bothers me. So I’ll usually check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn’t let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it is. Normal for me, anyway. \n\nI don't really pay attention to those standard charts or whatever they use at the clinic. I've got a frame that's built for moving, not for carrying extra bulk around. So... yeah, for my height and the kind of work I do, I think it's perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I mean, I can't even remember the last time I sat down and ate a big meal, let alone a bunch of food all at once. I just stick to my routine. Small amounts, mostly just enough to keep me going. Eating a lot at once would just... it would feel heavy, you know? It would slow me down on the job. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time when you were alone?\n\nPatient: No, not even when I'm alone. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise a lot to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. My job is pretty physical as it is. I move freight and materials all day, so I'm constantly on my feet and lifting things. That's really all the workout I get. I'm usually pretty beat by the time I clock out anyway, so, no.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight interfere with your daily activities or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does get to me. I mean, if I eat something like rice, or bread, or anything greasy... I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just stays on my mind. It keeps me ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word afraid, exactly, but... yeah, I definitely don't want to put on fat. \n\nLike, if I eat rice, or bread, or something greasy, I just... I can’t stop thinking about it. I feel like it’s going to just sit on me right away. It bothers me. So I’ll usually check the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn’t let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it is. Normal for me, anyway. \n\nI don't really pay attention to those standard charts or whatever they use at the clinic. I've got a frame that's built for moving, not for carrying extra bulk around. So... yeah, for my height and the kind of work I do, I think it's perfectly fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I mean, I can't even remember the last time I sat down and ate a big meal, let alone a bunch of food all at once. I just stick to my routine. Small amounts, mostly just enough to keep me going. Eating a lot at once would just... it would feel heavy, you know? It would slow me down on the job. So, no, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time when you were alone?\n\nPatient: No, not even when I'm alone. I mean, my eating habits don't really change whether there's people around or not. I just don't have the desire to eat like that. It's just... small amounts, like I said. That's all I need.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. Like I was saying, I don't eat heavy meals anyway, so there's really no reason I'd ever need to do something like that. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise a lot to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. My job is pretty physical as it is. I move freight and materials all day, so I'm constantly on my feet and lifting things. That's really all the workout I get. I don't go to a gym or run or anything on top of that just to burn calories. I'm usually pretty beat by the time I clock out anyway, so, no.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight interfere with your daily activities or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does get to me. I mean, if I eat something like rice, or bread, or anything greasy... I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just stays on my mind. And then the next morning, I always have to check the scale, or check how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of becoming overweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. I mean, like I was just saying... that's exactly why I get so worked up over eating bread or rice or anything greasy. The thought of it just sitting on my stomach and making me fat really scares me. That's why I have to do those checks with the scale and my belt every single morning. I just can't stand the idea of getting big.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is actually the shape and size that you see it as when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, when I look in the mirror... especially at my stomach and my waist... I don't know if I see what other people see. I just see that if I'm not smaller, if I don't look tight and lean, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. It's like my shape tells me if I'm keeping it together or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see your body as being the same shape and size as you see it when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, I don't go around asking the guys at the warehouse how they think I look. I just know what I see when I look at myself. So... yeah, I couldn't really tell you if they see the same thing or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what the scale says it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess so. A scale is just a scale, right? I don't really have any reason to think it's wrong or anything. It just gives me the number, and... well, I just go by that. So, yeah, I believe that's what I actually weigh.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I don't think I look underweight. My sister and my regular doctor, they keep telling me I'm in danger because of my size, but... I really think they're making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. They just don't understand it. I'm just keeping myself light, sharp, and ready for work. When you're moving freight all day at my age, you can't afford to be carrying extra bulk. So, no, I don't see myself as underweight, I just see a guy who's built for the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you see your body and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really think there's a difference. I just see what's there. When I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist, I just... I feel like if I’m not smaller and tight-looking, then I’m not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. So I don't think my eyes are playing tricks on me or anything, I just see exactly what I need to keep in check.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape and size are the same as what other people see when they look at you?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. I mean... I know my sister and the doctor think I'm too small, so I guess they must be seeing something different than I do. But I don't really spend time worrying about how other people are looking at me. I just know what I see, and to me, I just look like a guy who's staying lean for a physical job. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you exercise a lot to burn off calories?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. My job is pretty physical as it is. I move freight and materials all day, so I'm constantly on my feet and lifting things. That's really all the workout I get. I don't go to a gym or run or anything on top of that just to burn calories. I'm usually pretty beat by the time I clock out anyway, so, no.\n\nDoctor: Does thinking about gaining weight interfere with your daily activities or cause you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does get to me. I mean, if I eat something like rice, or bread, or anything greasy... I just can't stop thinking that it's going to sit on me right away. It just stays on my mind. And then the next morning, I always have to check the scale, or check how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go.\n\nDoctor: Are you terrified of becoming overweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. I mean, like I was just saying... that's exactly why I get so worked up over eating bread or rice or anything greasy. The thought of it just sitting on my stomach and making me fat really scares me. That's why I have to do those checks with the scale and my belt every single morning. I just can't stand the idea of getting big.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is actually the shape and size that you see it as when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, when I look in the mirror... especially at my stomach and my waist... I don't know if I see what other people see. I just see that if I'm not smaller, if I don't look tight and lean, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. It's like my shape tells me if I'm keeping it together or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people see your body as being the same shape and size as you see it when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, I don't go around asking the guys at the warehouse how they think I look. I just know what I see when I look at myself. So... yeah, I couldn't really tell you if they see the same thing or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what the scale says it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess so. A scale is just a scale, right? I don't really have any reason to think it's wrong or anything. It just gives me the number, and... well, I just go by that. So, yeah, I believe that's what I actually weigh.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I don't think I look underweight. My sister and my regular doctor, they keep telling me I'm in danger because of my size, but... I really think they're making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. They just don't understand it. I'm just keeping myself light, sharp, and ready for work. When you're moving freight all day at my age, you can't afford to be carrying extra bulk. So, no, I don't see myself as underweight, I just see a guy who's built for the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you see your body and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really think there's a difference. I just see what's there. When I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist, I just... I feel like if I’m not smaller and tight-looking, then I’m not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. So I don't think my eyes are playing tricks on me or anything, I just see exactly what I need to keep in check.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape and size are the same as what other people see when they look at you?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. I mean... I know my sister and the doctor think I'm too small, so I guess they must be seeing something different than I do. But I don't really spend time worrying about how other people are looking at me. I just know what I see, and to me, I just look like a guy who's staying lean for a physical job. If they see something else... well, I'm not really sure why.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body image is distorted or not accurate compared to how others see you?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't call it distorted. Like I said before, when I look in the mirror... mainly at my stomach and my waist... I just see what I need to see. I feel like if I'm not smaller and tight-looking there, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. I don't think my view is inaccurate, it's just... what I hold myself to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I don't go around asking the guys at the warehouse how they think I look. I just know what I see when I look at myself. So... yeah, I couldn't really tell you if they see the same thing or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is the same as what the scale says it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess so. A scale is just a scale, right? I don't really have any reason to think it's wrong or anything. It just gives me the number, and... well, I just go by that. So, yeah, I believe that's what I actually weigh.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look underweight when you see yourself in the mirror?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I don't think I look underweight. My sister and my regular doctor, they keep telling me I'm in danger because of my size, but... I really think they're making a much bigger deal out of it than it actually is. They just don't understand it. I'm just keeping myself light, sharp, and ready for work. When you're moving freight all day at my age, you can't afford to be carrying extra bulk. So, no, I don't see myself as underweight, I just see a guy who's built for the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a difference between how you see your body and how it actually is?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really think there's a difference. I just see what's there. When I look in the mirror, especially at my stomach and my waist, I just... I feel like if I’m not smaller and tight-looking, then I’m not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. So I don't think my eyes are playing tricks on me or anything, I just see exactly what I need to keep in check.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body shape and size are the same as what other people see when they look at you?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you exactly what other people see. I mean... I know my sister and the doctor think I'm too small, so I guess they must be seeing something different than I do. But I don't really spend time worrying about how other people are looking at me. I just know what I see, and to me, I just look like a guy who's staying lean for a physical job. If they see something else... well, I'm not really sure why.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body image is distorted or not accurate compared to how others see you?\n\nPatient: Well, no, I wouldn't call it distorted. Like I said before, when I look in the mirror... mainly at my stomach and my waist... I just see what I need to see. I feel like if I'm not smaller and tight-looking there, then I'm just not the disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. I don't think my view is inaccurate, it's just... what I hold myself to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your own body shape and size might be different from reality?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's different from reality, no. I know we've kind of gone over this, but... I just see what's actually there. When I look in the mirror, it's really just my stomach and my waist that I focus on. I just know that if I'm not smaller and tight-looking right there, then I'm losing my edge. I'm not the disciplined guy I was when I was working the warehouse floor every day. So, to me, it's not a different reality, it's just the standard I have to meet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be afraid of gaining weight if you were at a normal weight according to the standard charts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'm pretty sure I would be. Honestly, even if a chart says I'm normal, I just... I can't shake that feeling. Like, if I eat rice, or bread, or anything greasy, I just can't stop thinking about it. I feel like it's going to just sit on me right away. I always end up checking the scale or pulling at my belt the next morning, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. It's just always there in my head, no matter what a chart says.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would see yourself as fat even if you were at a healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I think I probably would. Like I keep saying... it really just comes down to what I see when I look in the mirror. If I weighed what people consider healthy, but I looked at my stomach and my waist and they weren't tight and small... I'd still feel fat. Or just soft, you know? If I'm not tight-looking right there, I just feel like I'm not that disciplined man I used to be on the warehouse floor. So, yeah, a healthy weight wouldn't really change how I see myself if my stomach wasn't right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
